# Patch Notes - PLAN v9.5: Stage 2, the shooter grows

**v9.5 (2026-11-28, after M51; replay-feedback-2026-11-27, ui-v4-pick-2026-11-27, ui-real-pixel-art-request):** **Stage 1 chains on its own, and the Pixel look gets its real UI.** S0 to S3 no longer wait for `play`: each one starts about a second after the AI's last line, and S0 hands the controls over with a slide that goes too fast until a wall stops it. Real choices (the body pick, the dash pick) still pause. From the S9 reveal, the Pixel look swaps the version rail for **the cabinet**: the game in an arcade frame with a slim marquee, a deck under it with the AI on camera and a dock per path, the dev-chat on the right, hearts and the dash ring on the player, and one corner plate with a skull and the bugs left. Packs open all at once, in rows. The owner approved the pixel mockup on 2026-11-28 (`.check/mockups/ui_v4/concept_pixel.html`). Built as M47.7a, M47.7b, then M48.6a to M48.6f. **Section 0.9 is the authority for v9.5.** The Stage 2 path preview (try a path's final form, then give a verdict) is not in v9.5; it is listed in section 9.

**v9.4 (2026-11-26, after M50.1b; stage1-phases-rework):** **Stage 1 and the game's phases are reworked.** The game boots on a dev desktop: the game window shows only the square, with the movement keys drawn under it and the square straining toward any key it cannot follow yet. **The AI talks from the first second** in the chat window beside it; **during a session its lines come in a small bubble on the game window**. **A session starts only when you type `play`** (or press Enter on the ghost `play`) in the chat. **There is no goal text**: a session ends when its fixed set of enemies is dead, or its script is done and a few seconds pass, or you die. **Between sessions the map does not reload**: the same room, you where you stood; only a death, a chapter or a resume reloads. After S2 the terminal becomes a proper chat (Blob in the header, bubbles, a patch card, quick replies). S3 is three kills; S4's walls grow in; S5 brings the new enemy much more often (owner: the crowd's base rate stays) and ends just after the gravity; S6 ends when its 28 are dead (the last few come to you); S7 is quicker and names each path (ARCADE, SURVIVAL, AUTO); Survival gets a revolver with its rounds above you. Built as M47.6a, b, c1, c2, d1, d2. **Section 0.8 is the authority for v9.4.** Design: `.check/stage1_rework/design.md`.

**v9.3 (2026-11-25, after M47.4; always-on-storytelling):** **the game is always on.** There is no end screen and no dev session between sessions: the AI talks in a **terminal docked to the right of the running game**. It shows a fake "thinking" wait, types its lines, asks its forks (number keys, with raw preview boxes over the frozen game), prints the patch, and its change lands on the game screen with a short hot-reload glitch; the next session starts when you move. **The only true pauses are chapters** (the stage card, S7's door and cook beat). The game **starts with no title**, straight into S0. The title appears at the Stage 1 → 2 door, and only from then on is it the boot screen. This is the **raw, pre-UI** terminal, built as M47.5a (hosting and the terminal beat) and M47.5b (story flow). The polished panel comes with M48.5; S9's `ui` reveal recolours the terminal. **Section 0.7 is the authority for v9.3.** Sections touched: header, 0.0, new 0.7, 1.16, 2, 3.2 (S7's door line 1 and first debrief line), 4, 5, 6 (M47.5a/b, M48.5 note), 9. Nothing up to M47.4 changes. Design (critiqued, with its defaults D1-D16 and open questions Q1-Q8): `.check/always_on/design.md`.

**v9.2 (2026-11-25, during M48a; owner's Stage 2 & 3 plan doc, stage-2-3-plan-doc-decisions):** (1) **one look for now, warm Pixel**: the other looks, the S8 try-on and `fork_look` are parked; the look arrives in S9 as a **scripted transformation** (one enemy first, then the rest during play, with visual bugs the AI fixes). (2) **One boss, the Big Square, tuned per path**; `fork_boss`, the Copycat and the Patcher are parked. (3) **At 50% HP the boss fight turns into a turn-based RPG** (the first bug caused by the final boss, not by the AI); weapons become abilities; a death in the RPG half retries **at the switch**. (4) **Path depth** gets its own design pass and milestone. Everything else in v9.1 stays (layout, HUD, the three enemies, gravity through the screen, the front door). **Section 0.6 is the authority for v9.2**; older text it overrides is marked "*v9.2:*" where it sits. Sections touched: header, 0.2, new 0.6, 1.7 (+1.7.1), 1.10, 1.12, new 1.17-1.18, 3.1, 3.2, 6, 7, 9. Nothing up to M48a changes.

**v9.1 (2026-11-25, after M47.2; owner-polish-vision):** a front door (title, options, pause, a polished dev chat) lands at the Stage 1 -> 2 door as M47.3-M47.4; the three looks become **code-drawn per-entity art** (Pixel / Minimal / Splat, voxel as Splat's fallback) in M48a-M48b, replacing Pixel / Neon / Paper and the "no per-entity art" rule; the UI styles (`fork_hud` kept) are built right after, in M48.5. Sections touched: header, 0.2-0.5, 1.7, 1.8, 1.12, new 1.16, 2, 3.2, 5, 6 (M47.3 on), 7, 8, 9. Nothing up to M47.2 changes.

**For:** the builder agent
**Goal of this build:** Stage 1 ends on "Next: what kind of shooter is it?". v9 answers it. The player plays **Stage 2, "The shooter" (6 sessions + a boss, ~30–40 min with reading)**. The AI builds three versions of the shooter in front of you and you keep one (**Arsenal**, **Auto** or **Survival**). Then it tries paint on you and you pick the look (**Pixel**, **Minimal** or **Splat**, each drawing every entity in code; v9.1). It builds levels, makes three new enemies (all three stay), rebuilds the screen, tries to give the game depth and pulls everything **through the screen** at you, builds a boss that does nothing, and finally builds a **boss that must be beaten** (a checkpoint with unlimited retries and AI help). Every session brings something new, and the AI stays the main force: it swaps, paints, edits and apologises, live.
**Engine:** Godot **4.7.x stable**, GDScript, 2D, unchanged.
**Roadmap:** `ROADMAP.md` **v3**, Phase 6 ("Stage 2: the shooter grows").
**Supersedes:** nothing is dropped. **PLAN v8** (Phase 5) stays in force unless this document changes it. Section 0 lists what changes. PLAN v8 is `PLAN.md` today; M43's step 0 archives it as `PLAN.v8.md` (marked "Phase 5, done") and installs this plan as `PLAN.md`, as ROADMAP v3 did with v7. "PLAN.v8.md" below means that archived file.

Milestones continue from **M43**. Owner gates continue from **G14**.

## 0. Read this first

### 0.0 Where we are (built)
- **Stage 1 is built and playtested:** M37–M42.2, tags up to `m42.2`. The campaign runs S0–S6 (v0.0 to v0.7.0) from a black screen to a small shooter. It has the mascot (3 bodies, a pick), the staged debrief, ~30 live reactions, the rule book (10 edits), the `wrong_variable` event, the dash pick (classic / grapple / teleport) and the dash-aim question. It ends on "To be continued".
- **The As-built record is in `PLAN.v8.md`**, sections 6.1–6.9 (M37 to M42.2): the real field names, cue syntax, line conditions, the rule book's owner layers, the slow-mo busy hold, the bot numbers and the reviews. v9 is written against that record, not against v8's original contracts. When this plan and v8 6.x disagree on how something works **today**, v8 6.x is right and this plan's change is the delta.
- **Last numbers (M42.2):** GUT 598/598. Both v6 hash compares 0 failures. Invincible chain (grapple+aim) `play=290.0`. The mortal chain (teleport+aim) won all 7 sessions on the first try. These are the "Stage 1 unchanged" references for M43–M48. They are too weak alone (the chain's `play=290.0` is mostly time goals, so a changed gun keeps it), so **before M43 a campaign hash is recorded at the `m42.2` tag**: `run_hash.gd --campaign --session=<id>` for s1_03–s1_06, per second (entity kinds, positions, kills), plus the tier-0 screenshot reference (5 frames, windowed, same machine). "The same lines" means the botrun lines with `real=` and `read≈` stripped.
- **G13 (owner, 2026-11-23):** "interactions were fun, gravity changes were good". The dash became a pick (M42.1), the bomber explodes on death with a bigger radius, and dash aim was added (M42.2). PLAYTEST 7a–7j were **not answered**, so their defaults stand (g13-open-questions). v9 re-asks them briefly at G14a.

### 0.1 Stage 2 in one paragraph
The prototype is a small game now (v0.7.0). The AI wants a real shooter but doesn't know which kind. So it builds **three quick versions inside one session** and lets you play each for ~45 s. You keep one. The other two go to the shelf ("I'll keep the other two in a drawer"), where Phase 8's final boss will find them (ROADMAP 2). From then on the game grows **inside your path**: a new gun every session (as a card, a level-up pick or a gun on the ground), the look you pick, levels in the shape you pick, the enemy you keep, the screen in the style you pick. The AI makes two "mistakes": it tries to add depth and everything falls toward your face, and it builds a boss that does nothing. Then the boss gets a personality (your pick), and you beat it to finish the stage. **Power stays in the session** (ROADMAP 1.9.7): each session starts you at the pistol again. The campaign adds guns, enemies and rules to the game, not stats to you.

- **Quick early steps:** S7 and S8 are the shortest full sessions (owner: early steps must be quick, "they are not really interesting"). Lengths grow toward ROADMAP 1.2's "5–12 min later" only from S10.
- **Length, against the owner's target:** the owner set the first campaign at **~2–3 h (~2 h 30)** (ROADMAP 2). Running total after v9: Stage 1 ≈ 7 min (target ~20; the 9–11 min gate was missed and "owner decides whether S6 grows" is **still unanswered**, g13-open-questions) + Stage 2 ≈ 30–40 min (target ~45) = **≈ 40–47 min of ~2 h 30**, with Stages 3–5 (target ~85 min) still to come. That is short. v9 does not guess which way the owner wants it: **the M43 report asks one line**: "Stage 1 is ~7 min against ~20 and Stage 2 is planned at 30–40 against 45. Grow now (a session in a stage's middle, ROADMAP 2) or later?" Session lengths are data (`SessionDef.goal` / `duration`), so S7–S13 are built at 3.1's lengths by default and retuned only after the answer.
- **Forks, counted against ROADMAP 2 (the per-stage list) and 1.6 ("about 10–12"):** ROADMAP 2 gives Stage 2 four forks (path, level layout, HUD style, shooter boss) and puts the look at the end of Stage 1 and the enemy type in Stage 1 (built as `fork_enemy`). v9 asks **5**: path (S7), look (S8, moved from Stage 1, 0.3), layout (S9), HUD (S11), boss (S12). **There is no second enemy fork**: the three new enemies are announced and all stay (1.9). The campaign: Stage 1 = mascot, dash (owner-added at G13, after the budget was set), enemy (+ dash aim, a 2-card follow-up) · Stage 2 = 5 · Stage 3 = 3 (RPG path, RPG enemies, RPG boss) · Stage 4 = 1 ("what do we keep?") · Stage 5 = 1 (the final boss's form) = **13** (14 with dash aim). That is one over the budget, because of the owner's own dash fork. **The M43 report asks one line:** "13 forks against 10–12. Drop `fork_hud` (the AI picks the style from your play, announced) to reach 12?" Default if unanswered: keep it, as ROADMAP 2 lists it. **Answered (v9.1, owner-polish-vision): keep it** ("UI polish with 3 styles"); built in M48.5. Debriefs with a choice: S7, S8, S9, S11, S12 (5 of 7; S10 and S13 have none), closer to ROADMAP 1.2's "in some sessions".

### 0.4 Principles that hold (owner decisions; memory wins over docs)
- **The AI is the main force** (final-vision; ROADMAP 1.3). Every session has at least one thing the AI changes live (the "AI does" column in 0.2; the check table in 3.4). The finished game must not feel "natural".
- **Nothing exists by default. The AI adds each piece live and says so in the session it adds it** (final-vision, M39.1/M39.2; owner: "audit everything default-on"). The path's growth piece, the new HUD, new guns and new enemies arrive by **scripted** cue with a line in the same session (never only by a reaction, which can be dropped by priority or cooldown), never silently through the previous debrief's notes. **A weapon joins the session's offer, drop and crate pools only when its `give` or `say` cue fires**, so no pick, drop or crate hands it out before the AI has said it (tested). Section 3.5 lists every new rule or mechanic with the cue and line that brings it, or why it is invisible.
- **Choices only for big run-start features** (dev-session-big-only). Path, look, layout and HUD change the game from its first second. **`fork_boss` does not:** it shapes only S13. It rests on a later owner decision instead (final-vision: "choices cover mechanics, visuals, UI/UX, enemies, **bosses**, level design"; ROADMAP 2 lists the shooter boss as a Stage 2 fork). New guns, the splitter, the three S10 enemies and the edits are **announced by the AI, never picked from 3**. The AI pitches first, then offers the cards (g13-feedback's dash pattern: "I think … what do you want?").
- **In-run picks are the path, not a dev-session fork.** Arsenal's card picks and Auto's level-up picks happen *inside* play, offer 1 of 3, and reset every session (ROADMAP 1.7: "card picks and card upgrades" / "level-up picks"). They use a new **`PickOffer`** (weapon ids + ranks) and **`PickPanel`** (a `PromptPanel` subclass) and a small path meter, never `Offer` / `AiDirector.on_choice` (which apply a Mutation to the stack, bump the build number and write patch notes): they never offer rule edits or patches, never touch `RunState`'s stack or `build_number`, and never ship anything to the save. This is the one place v9 shows 3-way picks outside a debrief. It is **asked early, at G14a**, with a fallback ready (section 9).
- **Picks are exclusive and permanent** (g11-feedback, A): picking shelves the others. The shelf is stored so Phase 8's final boss can use it. Rework stays parked.
- **Content = sum, not product** (ROADMAP 1.6). Paths, looks, layouts, enemies, HUDs and bosses are independent slots. Weapons are shared by all three paths. A path is **one `PathDef` plus at most one small component, ≤ 250 path-only lines** (reported per milestone; going over needs the owner's OK). HUD styles are tokens over shared widgets. **Looks are code-drawn skins over one set of bodies (v9.1): visuals never touch the simulation, and menus never step it.** Bosses read the player's weapons and dash instead of having path versions.
- **Power stays in the session** (ROADMAP 1.9.7). Weapon ranks, levels, the hand and pickups reset at every session start. Saved: the path, the weapon *pool* each session lists, and the look, layout, enemy, HUD and boss answers. Asked at G14a (section 9).
- **The AI announces before it edits** (ROADMAP 1.4). Every new edit and the new event warn first. The S7 path swaps, the S8 look try-on and the S9 repaint are announced the same way (a line, a 1.5 s warning, then the change).
- **Story never stalled; bosses are the only gates** (ROADMAP 1.2, 1.9.5). A normal session still offers Move on after a death. The boss never offers Move on. Retries are unlimited and restart at the boss. Help is offered after losses, but the boss still has to fall.
- **Fun before meta:** every session is fun under `--mute-ai`, on every path.
- **Readable apologies** (final-vision: "not too meta"). The depth gag is "I tried to make it 3D. It came toward you instead of away.", not "I flipped the z-axis".
- **Stage 2 does not spend later events' jokes.** UI overload, flicker and the fake "Not Responding" crash belong to Stage 4 (ROADMAP 2). No Stage 2 boss, HUD beat or line uses them.
- **Bots read pacing, not fun** (bot-runs-as-tuning-proxy). Every number in section 6 says whether it is a bot number or needs a human. **Mortal-bot numbers (deaths, time to death, boss tries) are reported with hit kinds, never pass/fail**: a mortal bot is not a model of human skill, and a miss means checking the bot policy first, never retuning the game to it. Hard gates stay only on invincible pacing, determinism, hashes, the telegraph and dry-time rules and "0 hits during the event". Check kills per run first when numbers jump.
- **Lines: I draft every line, the owner edits for tone** (ROADMAP 6.7). A lines file (`campaign/lines_stage2.md`, generated from the session data: 3.2 + 3.3 + the fork cards) ships with the G14a build for a tone pass before M54. Line-timing tests read the data, so an edit is a data change, never a test rewrite.
- **Repo is public** (github-repo): commit per milestone with a tag, as before. Push only when the owner asks.

### 0.5 How Stage 2 can fail (what the milestone order retires first)
| # | Risk | How v9 retires it | Retired at | If it fails |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | **The three paths feel like one game** (especially Arsenal and Auto, which both pick 1 of 3) | The paths differ on **three axes** (1.5): *what your hands do*, *where power comes from*, *what kills you*. A reported **play fingerprint** and degenerate-strategy **probes** (4) show it; the owner's play at G14a decides | M45–M47 → **G14a** | M47.1 re-spikes the weakest path before any content is stacked on it; for Arsenal vs Auto, the fallback in 9 (the AI deals Auto's picks) |
| R2 | **In-run picks read as the dev-session picks the owner rejected** | Styled as in-game cards, never the mascot's debrief cards; never rule edits; Survival has none; asked directly | **G14a** | Arsenal cards come as drops you walk over; Auto's level-ups are auto-chosen by taste with a line (9) |
| R3 | **The looks read as programmer art, or Splat reads as mush** (v9.1; was "three tints") | Every colour from palette roles; every entity drawn by the look's skin; the Pixel sheet sent to the owner early (M48a); silhouette gates; luminance, contrast and per-pixel distinctness checks on captures | M48a / M48b → **G14b** | `look_voxel` for Splat (1.7's decision rule); `look_terminal` for any other look (9) |
| R4 | **The boss is a damage sponge** | Anti-sponge rules (1.10): every phase has a window or a mechanic that ends it, dry time ≤ 8 s, phases 25–45 s for the bot (forced loadouts), a `max_seconds` backstop that only moves to the next phase (P3 opens a damage window instead). Built as a **vertical slice** before S12/S13 | M49 → **G14b** | the boss keeps P1 + P3 only (~70 s) and `fork_boss` drops to one form; the checkpoint stays |
| R5 | **Scope** (5 forks × 3 answers) | One foundation; each milestone adds one session plus one fork; the path line budget; a cut order (6) | every milestone | cut order |
| R6 | **Auto crowds tank the frame rate** | spatial-hash separation (path sessions only), the pickup cap, separate projectile budgets, a bench gate | M45b | Auto cap 100 enemies, HP ×0.8 |
| R7 | **Gravity through the screen reads as a bug, or kills you** | presentation only (frozen play, proxies), warned, clock held, the AI's panic as the punchline, a gentle variant | M52 | shorten to squash + crack + pop back (3 s) |

### 0.6 v9.2: what changed on 2026-11-25 (this section wins over older text)
The owner shared a written plan for Stages 2 and 3 and answered the open points the same day (memory: stage-2-3-plan-doc-decisions).

**Decisions (owner):**
1. **Still in:** the layout pick, the HUD pick, the three S10 enemies, gravity through the screen, the front door, Stages 4-5.
2. **Looks: warm Pixel first, alone.** "We'll try everything" later (candidates: neon pixel, cartoon cutout, minimal, splat). With one look there is no pick: `fork_look` and S8's try-on are **parked** and come back, with mock-up cards, when a second look exists.
3. **The look arrives as a scripted transformation** (S9, 1-2 min): one invulnerable enemy turns from a placeholder into its sprite, the AI celebrates its first asset, the other entities follow during play, some with visual bugs the AI fixes fast. The player, the arena and the UI follow (1.7.1).
4. **One boss to begin with** (more later): the **Big Square**. `fork_boss`, the Copycat and the Patcher are **parked**. **The boss is tuned per path**: slower and with longer warnings on Survival and Arsenal (fewer dodges), full speed on Auto (1.18).
5. **The switch:** at ~50% HP the game freezes and the fight becomes a **turn-based RPG**. The AI is confused: it did not do this. Weapons and upgrades convert to turn-based abilities, and a short guided sequence lets the player finish the boss (1.18).
6. **The switch is the final boss's doing.** The final boss is the source of the game's bugs, and this is its first visible act. Stage 1-2's earlier accidents (gravity sideways, gravity through the screen) stay the AI's own mistakes, so the tone shift is felt here. **This is the one change in Stage 2 that the AI does not announce** (ROADMAP 1.4 covers the AI's edits; this is not one).
7. **A death in the RPG half retries at the switch.** A death in the shooter half retries the fight from its start.
8. **Path depth is important** (fusion for Arsenal, spell evolutions for Auto, tactical Survival). The 250-line path budget becomes a reported number, not a limit (1.17).
9. **Stage 3 stays RPG only** (ROADMAP). The shooter is kept in mind through the converted kit and the mascot's lines, and returns in Stage 4.

**What v9.2 overrides:**
| Section | v9.1 | v9.2 |
|---|---|---|
| 0.2, 3.1, 3.2 (S8) | the AI tries three looks on you, then `fork_look` | the AI says the game still looks like a prototype and goes to learn to draw; no pick |
| 0.2, 3.1, 3.2 (S9) | a 1.5 s repaint at 3 s; `kills 40` | the transformation (1.7.1); `survive 150` |
| 1.7 | three looks (Pixel / Minimal / Splat), try-on, distinctness checks | Pixel only; per-id reveal, morph and scripted bugs; Minimal, Splat and the 3-look checks parked |
| 1.10, 3.2 (S12-S13) | three bosses, a pick, 3 shooter phases | the Big Square: 2 shooter phases to 50%, then the switch and the RPG half (1.18) |
| 1.12 | 5 forks in Stage 2 | **3**: path, layout, HUD (11 across the campaign) |
| 1.4, 0.4 | a path is ≤ 250 path-only lines | reported only; path depth designed in 1.17 |
| 3.2 (S13's last lines) | "I'm a bit bored of shooting" / "What if we tried turns?" | the AI did not write turns and wants to find out what did |
| 6 | M48b, M49, M50.1, M53 | rewritten (see the table) |

**Parked, not dropped (kept in this file as written):** Minimal and Splat (1.7's table), `Look.try_on` and `fork_look`, the Copycat and the Patcher with `fork_boss` (1.10's table), the Big Square's phase 3 split.

### 0.7 v9.3: always on (this section wins over older text)
*Owner (2026-11-25): "instead of having gaming session then dev session, the game will 'always' be on." Memory: always-on-storytelling. The owner liked the "before UI work" mock-up: a black terminal, monospace, `[14:02] cursor@patchnotes (happy)` lines, cyan `$ build` / `$ patch apply` / `$ hot-reload` lines, a "cursor is thinking ⠋" line before a change lands, numbered yellow choices and a `you>` input, docked right of the game.*

**Rules (owner):** the game screen is always on; no title at the start; a session's end is a terminal beat, not a screen; the only true pauses are chapters.

**Defaults (mine, to confirm at the next playtest):**
- **Forks (session picks)** are answered in the terminal with 1-3 while the arena is frozen at its goal, with raw preview boxes over the game. **In-run picks** (Arsenal cards, Auto level-ups) stay in the game as today.
- **Next session** starts on your first move or shot after the hot-reload (the arena is visible meanwhile).
- **Death:** the AI reacts, then "restarting in 3 . 2 . 1" (`[1]` now, `[2]` = Move on). A new attempt from the session start, as today's Retry, same telemetry; it never auto-restarts twice without input.
- **The terminal** is on the right and always visible, apart from chapters.
- **Esc** still opens M47.3's pause menu over the game view. Before the front door the raw pause line also has quit game, fullscreen, volume and less flashing.
- **The mascot's body stays in the game corner**; its words go to the terminal; no bubble.

**How it works:** a `GameHost` scene holds the game in a SubViewport with a fixed 1280x720 2D rect beside the terminal (base 1760x720). Each session is still one fresh, pausable `main.tscn` instance; the host swaps instances under a 0.25 s glitch instead of reloading the scene. `DebriefScreen` keeps its logic, API and key handling and drives the terminal instead of its own screen. Stage cards put the host in chapter mode.

**What v9.3 overrides:**

| Section | v9.2 | v9.3 |
|---|---|---|
| 2 (debrief) | full-screen debrief; polished dev chat from Stage 2 | terminal beat beside the frozen arena; the polished chat moves to M48.5 |
| 2 (front door), 1.16 routing | raw start screen before S7, title from S7 | no start screen before S7; title from S7 as before |
| 1.16 (first-launch question) | the start screen's panel | an installer prompt in the terminal before S0 |
| 1.16 (raw pause) | Esc resume, Q quit to start | + F / -/+ / L; Q quits the game before the front door |
| 3.2 S7 door beat line 1 | "...the front door needs work." | "...this game needs a front door." |
| 3.2 S7 debrief | "happy: Like the new chat? I redecorated." | cut |
| 4 (bot) | chains through scene reloads | the full chain goes through the host; `run_hash --shell` added |
| 6 | M47.4 then M48a | + M47.5a, M47.5b after M47.4 |

**Determinism:** the simulation of every session is unchanged; every campaign hash ref holds, hosted and unhosted.

**Defaults taken from the design (D1-D16, stated to the owner):** D1 forks in the terminal, in-run picks in the game; D2 death countdown (M47.5b); D3 no title before the front door; D4 body in the corner, words in the terminal; D5 terminal right, 480 px at the base, ≥ 360 px, game column scaled on its own; D6 Esc over the game view only, refused during a beat; D7 0.25 s glitch, fresh instance, wait for the first move; D8 a quit mid-beat resumes at the session start; D9 S7 lines (M47.5b); D10 clock 14:00 + 3 min per session + real minutes; D11 raw pause keys (M47.5b); D12 New game before the door = `--new-game` (M47.5b); D13 installer prompt (M47.5b); D14 `ai@patchnotes` until the S3 body pick, then the body's name, renamed live; D15 S9 recolours the terminal (M47.5b); D16 player-facing cmd rows, quiet edits silent (M47.5b). **Open (defaults taken):** Q1 a new attempt, not a revive; Q2 frozen at the goal; Q4 the title stays from S7; Q6 quiet between lines; Q7 no pause menu during a beat; Q8 pick-based name.

### 0.8 v9.4: Stage 1 and the phases (this section wins over older text)
*Owner (2026-11-26), memory stage1-phases-rework; mockups approved in ui-mockup-feedback-v3 (the terminal window "perfect", the chat upgrade "really good").*

**Rules (owner):** first boot = the terminal window + the game window with only the square, the movement keys and the square's strain; the AI talks from the start; chat between sessions, a bubble during them; a session starts on `play` in the chat; no goal text (the AI calls each ending in the bubble), sessions end on all fixed enemies dead / script done + a few seconds / death; no map reload between sessions unless an event needs it. Per session: S2 as is; S3 3 kills; S4 walls spawn in; S5 much faster spawns, ends 4-5 s after the gravity or all dead; S6 story kept, ends when all 25-30 are dead; S7 a title per path (ARCADE / SURVIVAL / AUTO), quicker; Survival fewer enemies, a revolver, ammo above the player.

**Owner answers (2026-11-26, design section 10; they win over the defaults below):** S5 raises only the **new enemy type**'s share and rate (the crowd's base rate stays); **Enter on the ghost `play` is fine** (typing `play` / `p` still works); the chat header names the AI from the moment the chat appears after S2, so **the body pick (`fork_mascot`) moves from S3's end to S2's end** (the chat upgrade beat asks it; saves past S3 keep their answer; a save between S2 and S3 gets the pick at its next beat).

**Defaults (mine, to confirm at the next playtest):** play = `play` / `p` / Enter on the ghost (armed once the AI's lines are typed) / the quick reply; the movement keys never type at an empty prompt, they strain the square; the keys shown by layout (ZQSD on AZERTY, WASD on QWERTY); the bubble is the mascot's own, 2 rows; the death countdown is gone (Enter / 1 = again); the next session is a fresh instance built invisibly with your position carried; enemies and shots left at a session end pop out in the beat; a silent safety cap on every enemy-count session; S8 / S9 end on a per-path fixed set; the revolver is Survival's start weapon everywhere; "Arcade" is the Arsenal path's display name.

|---|---|---|
| 0.7 next session | starts on your first move or shot | starts on `play` in the chat, also the first session after a boot |
| 0.7 death | "restarting in 3 . 2 . 1" | again / move on; Enter / 1 = again |
| 0.7 mascot | words in the terminal, no bubble | bubble during play, chat between |
| 0.7 / 6.14 hot-reload | a 0.25 s glitch and a fresh session at the start | invisible (position carried); the glitch only on death, chapter, restart, resume |
| 1.2 goals | survive / kills / stop / overrun | + `clear` / `script` / `ends`; no campaign session shows goal text |

| 6 | M48.5 next | M47.6a, b, c1, c2, d1, d2 first |

**Determinism:** a session is (seed, data, answers, start point); refs are recorded from `player_start`; the chain hash proves the carry. Stage 1 cold refs move once (M47.6c1), the chain ref is born in M47.6c2, S7 and Survival refs move once (M47.6d1), Arcade / Auto S8 / S9 once (M47.6d2).

### 0.9 v9.5: Stage 1 chains, and the cabinet (this section wins over older text)
*Owner (2026-11-27 replay, 2026-11-27 UI pick, 2026-11-28 approval of the pixel mockup and answers). Mockup: `.check/mockups/ui_v4/concept_pixel.html` (main checkout only; it is the visual reference for every M48.6 milestone).*

#### 0.9.1 Stage 1 chains on its own (M47.7a, M47.7b)

**Rules (owner):** typing `play` between sessions breaks the flow. **S0, S1, S2 and S3 start on their own.** The story is enough: the breather is the AI's own lines. A real choice still pauses the game (the body pick at S2's end, the dash picks at S3's end, the death ask). In S0 the player cannot move; the AI talks and hands the controls over with no `play` step. **When the controls land, the square slides too fast in the last direction the player tried, until it hits a wall.**

**Owner answers (2026-11-28):** the next session starts **when the AI finishes talking** (no fixed countdown). "Skip to latest" on the title screen uses **a throwaway save**, never the real one (it applies when that entry is built; the title milestone owns it).

**How it works:**
- `SessionDef` gains `auto_start: bool` (default false). It is true on `s1_00_nothing`, `s1_01_move`, `s1_02_enemies` and `s1_03_shooting`. Nothing branches on a stage number.
- `GameHost.hold()` still freezes the session at tick 0 (the HANDOFF pause) and types the session's `before_play` lines. With `auto_start`, the chat input does not open a gate. The host releases the hold **1.0 s after the terminal stops typing** (`GameHost.AUTO_START_DELAY`, real time, presentation only). The ghost `play`, the hint and the play pill are not shown for these sessions.
- A key press during the lines does not skip them (movement keys still strain the square in S0). Enter skips the typing, as today; the 1.0 s then runs from there.
- The four sessions' `before_play` lines lose their "type play" wording. The AI's words are the owner's to edit; the builder writes drafts in the same lowercase S0 voice.
- **Where the gate stays:** S4 and later; a boot or Continue into any session **except S0** (the player may not be at the keyboard); "move on" after a death. A restart ("again") keeps today's carry with no gate.
- **The slide (M47.7b).** The start point of a session gains one optional field, the **last tried direction** (a unit vector snapped to 8 ways, or zero). `SessionRunner` already counts tries; it now also remembers the last one. When `movement` is granted to a player whose session start carries a tried direction (S0 to S1), the player moves in that direction at `Tuning.handover_slide_mult` (3.0) times the normal speed and ignores input, until a wall blocks it or `Tuning.handover_slide_cap` passes (2.0 s; first 1.2 s, raised at M47.7b on the owner's word "until it hits a wall": the cap is a safety only, every slide from S1's start ends on a wall). The AI then says one line ("...too fast. fixing.") and the speed is normal. With a zero direction nothing happens.
- The slide is real movement (it changes where S1 starts for a human), so it lives in gameplay code, reads no Palette or Skins, and draws no RNG.

**Determinism:** the hold is hash-neutral today (`run_hash.gd --handoff=`), and the auto release only replaces the key press that ends it. Tool and bot hosts never gate, as before. **Gate for M47.7a: no ref changed.** For the slide (corrected at M47.7b; the first text here said bots never try a key in S0, which was wrong): **bots do try keys in S0.** With no enemy the default bot policy steers in a slow circle, and `SessionRunner` counts that input as tries (the bot has fired S0's `tried 1` cue since M47.6a). So a bot ends S0 with a tried direction and **slides in S1**. The tool chain mirrors the game: `run_hash --chain` carries the direction through the same capture (`GameHost.capture_tried`), and `--start=<x>,<y>,<dx>,<dy>` replays a chained session cold. The chain ref therefore moves from S1 on, and **it is re-recorded on purpose in `.check/ref_m47_7b/`** (owner OK, 2026-11-28), with any S1+ campaign ref that moves. A start that carries no direction (every cold run) must not move. **Gate for M47.7b: no cold ref changed; the chain replays against `ref_m47_7b`, and each chained session replays cold from its printed start.**

**Tests (must fail without the change):** an `auto_start` session releases 1.0 s after the terminal is quiet, with no gate, no ghost and no pill; S4 still gates; a boot into S1 gates but a boot into S0 does not; a fork at S2's end still waits for its answer before the chain goes on; a scripted "left" try in S0 makes S1 start with a slide that ends at the left wall and ignores input meanwhile; a zero direction is a no-op; the chain hash and S0-S3 campaign hashes compare with 0 failures. `test_play_gate.gd` keeps its gate tests on S4.

#### 0.9.2 The cabinet: the Pixel look's UI (M48.6a to M48.6f)

**Rules (owner):** the UI must read as part of the pixel game, not as a dev overlay. The layout is the merged pick: frame A without bulbs, chat, host camera and package from C, open-all from B, card faces from A. **Before S9 nothing changes**: Stage 1 and S7-S8 keep today's look, and the cabinet arrives with the S9 reveal (`skin ui`), where the version rail arrives today. The cabinet **replaces the version rail** (M48.5a/b); the flat look (`--skins=off`, tier 0) keeps today's desktop and HUD.

**The layout (design canvas 1600 x 900, as `RailLayout` today; all rects in design px):**

| Piece | Rect | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| Cabinet | 12, 12, 1196, 876 | the frame, side trims, no bulbs |
| Marquee | 34, 24, 1152, 42 | stage, session, path name, the version plate |
| View | 34, 78, 1152, 648 | the game (16:9), the CRT edge |
| Deck | 34, 748, 1152, 128 | host camera, caption, the path dock |
| Chat | 1218, 12, 370, 876 | the dev-chat |

**On the view:** hearts and the dash ring are drawn on the player (Survival: hearts under the feet, rounds above, the battery beside). The **corner plate** sits at the view's top left: a skull and the bugs left to the session's goal, counting down (Survival: a clock and the time left). It hides when the session has no count. The Auto XP bar runs along the view's bottom edge and gems fly into it.

**In the deck:** the AI's body on camera at the left, with its mood plate and a caption that shows every line (also during picks). The dock takes the rest: **Arcade** shows the two-card hand (active tag, rank chip, fuse link) and the package with its xN badge and its kills gauge; **Auto** shows weapon cartridges and passive tokens with READY and MAX marks; **Survival** shows the revolver and its pickups.

**The chat:** one pixel dev-chat (header with the AI's name and the version, a 2-line build log, bubbles, dividers, PATCH and HOTFIX slips, reply pills, the input). The "terminal (legacy)" tab is removed in the Pixel look.

**Packs and cards:** one press opens every stacked pack. The packs fly out, shake and tear together; each deals a row of cards; the front row is live and the rows behind wait, smaller and dimmed; the pick flies into its hand slot and the next row comes forward. Past 4 packs the rest show as "+N more". Card faces say the tier by frame and ribbon (plum common, gold rare, holo epic, prism-striped fuse). **Owner (2026-11-28):** no rotating rays or halos anywhere; no banner on top of a card; **a rare or epic card flips as fast as a common one**, with a bigger burst (an epic adds a short flash and a shake). The fuse and the evolve keep their moment (the two cards meet, a flash, the result slams in), without rays.

**Art rules:** one pixel grid, 3 screen px per art px at the design size (the rail's `art_px` is 2 today; the cabinet uses 3). Frames are code-drawn 9-slices; all text uses the pixel font at sizes that sit on the grid; no gradients, rounded corners or blurs. Colours go through Palette; grids live under `systems/skins/art/` or `ui/` art files as today; no image files. The mockup's kits (`.check/mockups/ui_v4/pixel_art/*.js`: guns, pack, frames, fx) are the source to port, grid for grid. Every tween, flash, shake, flip and spark respects `reduce_flashing` (the mockup's toggle shows the fade-only version).

**Known gaps in the mockup that the build fixes (review findings, 2026-11-27):** small text off the grid (use on-grid sizes only); the fit scale snaps to whole screen px per art px where it can; the hand slots use the splash art, not the small icons; the pistol and laser grips need contrast on the art window; a maxed Auto weapon is not offered again.

**Determinism:** presentation only. Pack contents are still drawn from `pick_rng`, same order, same count per pack, at the same tick as today; open-all only changes when they are shown and picked. **Gate for every M48.6 milestone: `check.ps1 -BotRuns` and the hash gates, no ref changed.** If open-all cannot keep the draw order (for example a pick changes what a later pack may offer and today's draw happens after the pick), M48.6d stops and reports before any ref moves.

**Milestones (each ends playable; each is one PR; windowed captures compared with the mockup):**

| # | Milestone | What it builds | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| M47.7a | **Stage 1 chains** | `auto_start`, the timed release, the S0-S3 lines | GUT, no ref changed |
| M47.7b | **The handover slide** | the tried direction in the start point, the slide, the AI's line | GUT, no cold ref changed; the chain ref re-recorded on purpose (`ref_m47_7b`, owner OK) |
| M48.6a | **The cabinet frame** | `CabinetLayout` and `CabinetFrame` in place of the rail at `skin ui`: frame, marquee, view, empty deck, chat column rect; the frames kit ported; window base and mouse mapping | hashes, captures |
| M48.6b | **The view's HUD and the host** | hearts and dash ring on the player, the corner plate, the camera with the caption and mood plate; the rail's KILLS / HP / DASH modules go | hashes, captures |
| M48.6c | **The chat** | the pixel dev-chat in the chat column, no legacy tab | hashes, captures |
| M48.6d | **The Arcade dock and packs** | hand, package with badge and gauge, open-all rows, the new card faces and pack art, the reveals, the fuse moment; the guns and pack kits ported | hashes (see Determinism), captures |

**What v9.5 overrides:**

**Defaults (mine, to confirm at the next playtest):** the gate stays from S4 on; a Continue into S1-S3 gates once; the slide is 3x speed with a 2.0 s safety cap; the slide's line; the corner plate counts the session's fixed set (a `clear` goal) or its kills goal; the marquee shows "STAGE n · Sxx · title"; in the flat look nothing changes.
